1 Section 302 of the National Security Act of 1947, as amended, is uncodified as to the Army. The substance of that section as it pertains to the Army is codified in revised sections 303 and 305. 

2 50:741 is codified, as it is applicable to the Army, in revised section 513; and, as it is applicable to the 

Air Force, in revised section 523. 

10:291f-2 is applicable only to the Air Force and is codified in revised section 1213. 10:1393 (words of 

1st proviso of 1st paragraph after 9th semicolon) is applicable only to the Air Force and is codified in revised section 1909. 

• Section 22 of the Act of Mar. 4, 1925, ch. 536, as amended (34:821), provides that the Naval Reserve 

Officers' Training Corps shall be operated under regulations which, insofar as may be practicable, conform to the provisions of sections 40-53 of the National Defense Act, as amended (10:354, 364, 365, 381-387a, 389, 441, 444, and 536). That act also provides that the Secretary of the Navy shall have the same powers, with respect to the Naval Reserve Officers' Training Corps, as are conferred upon the Secretary of the Army, with respect to the Reserve Officers' Training Corps, by those sections of the National Defense 

Act. 10:364, cited in 34: 821, has been repealed, and the source credits for 10:444 have been transferred to 

10:387a. 10:365 and 536, though based upon section 40b of the National Defense Act, do not deal with the 

Reserve Officers' Training Corps. 10:442, based upon section 47d of the National Defense Act, established the Citizens' Military Training Camps. Although these camps are not part of the Reserve Officers' 

Training Corps, 34:821 may have been intended to make these provisions applicable to the Navy. 10:388, based upon the Act of Sept. 8, 1916, ch. 478, 39 Stat. 853, though not enacted as part of the National Defense Act, by its terms modifies section 47c of the National Defense Act (10:387). 10:390, based upon the 

Act of June 5, 1920, ch. 240, 41 Stat. 967, amends, by implication, section 47 of the National Defense Act 

10:486a, 1055-1060, 1091-1, 1092b, 1092c, 1149a, and 1162 are applicable only to the Army and are codified in revised sections 3625, 3627, 3633, 3637, 3639, 3643, 3647, 3651, 3653, and 3657. • Section 12 of the Armed Services Procurement Act of 1947 (5:219b, 412b, and 6260; and 41:161) provides that the Secretary of the Navy shall have the same authority as the Secretary of the Army with respect to Army contracts under 5:218; and that the Secretaries of the Army and the Air Force shall have the same authority as the Secretary of the Navy with respect to emergency purchases abroad under 34:568. 

10:311 provides that Army and Air Force contracts for aircraft shall be subject to all the provisions that apply to Navy aircraft contracts under 34:496 and 496b. 

The provisions of 31:222c are made applicable to the Navy by 31:222e. The provisions of 31:223b and 223c are made applicable to the Navy by 31:223d. The provisions of 31:2220, 223b, and 223c are made applicable to the Coast and Geodetic Survey and the Public Health Service while serving with the Navy by 31:222h. The provisions of 31:224d, 224e, 224g, and 224i are made applicable to the Coast

Guard while serving with the Navy by 31:224h. The fourth paragraph of section 424 (a) of the Act of 

Aug. 2, 1946, ch. 753, 60 Stat. 846, though uncodified, affects 31:223b and 223c. The substance of the cited uncodified provisions of the Act of Aug. 2, 1946, as it pertains to the Army and Air Force, is codified in revised section 5003. The Act of Aug. 4, 1949, ch. 393, 63 Stat. 495, in enacting title 14 of the United States 

Code into positive law for the Coast Guard, executed the provisions of 31:222g by restating the provisions of 31:222c, 223b, and 223c in 14:490 and 645.

Section 1 of the Act of Aug. 3, 1950, ch. 537, 64 Stat. 408, as restated by the first paragraph of section 3 of the Act of June 19, 1951, ch. 144, Title I, 65 Stat. 88, suspends generally all laws imposing restrictions on the authorized personnel strength of any component of the armed forces until July 31, 1954. That act specifically suspends the restrictions of 10:20j, 316a, and 481 which are applicable to the Army; 5:627a and 10:20s which are applicable to the Air Force; 34: 105a, 151, 625h, and 691 which are applicable to the Navy; and 50 Appendix 

452 which is applicable to each of the armed forces. The substance of the Act of Aug. 3, 1950, as restated, as it pertains to 5:627a and 10:20j, 20s, 316a, and 481; and 50 App. 452 is codified in revised sections 1103, 1109, 1115, 1117, 1125, 1129, 1133, and 1137.
